All functions are `#[inline]` where it makes sense to do so, and\n//! significant branches are annotated with `#[cold]` to let the optimizer\n//! keep hot-paths small.\n\nuse std::{\n    fmt,\n    fs::File,\n    io::Read,\n    path::{Path, PathBuf},\n    sync::atomic::{AtomicUsize, Ordering},\n    time::{Duration, Instant},\n};\n\nuse anyhow::{anyhow, Context, Result};\nuse once_cell::sync::Lazy;\nuse parking_lot::RwLock;\nuse serde::de::DeserializeOwned;\nuse serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse thiserror::Error;\nuse tracing::{debug, error, info, instrument};\n\n/// VisuTility's generic error type for utility-layer operations.\n///\n/// We prefer using [`anyhow::Error`] at the top-level application\n/// boundary to allow for heterogeneous sources, but within the\n/// utility layer we capture the most common failure categories so that\n/// callers can still pattern-match when they need to.\n#[derive(Debug, Error)]\npub enum UtilError {\n    #[error(\"I/O error: {0}\")]\n    Io(#[from] std::io::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"serialization error: {0}\")]\n    Serde(#[from] serde_json::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"configuration not found at path `{0}`\")]\n    ConfigNotFound(PathBuf),\n\n    #[error(\"task failed: {0}\")]\n    TaskFailed(String),\n}\n\n/// Generic wrapper around an [`Instant`] which logs the elapsed time at\n/// **drop**.  The measurement is therefore *scoped*; as soon as the guard\n/// goes out of scope, latency is emitted through the `tracing` facade.\n///\n/// ```no_run\n/// use visu_tility_utils::ScopedTimer;\n/// let _g = ScopedTimer::new(\"heavy_initialisation\");\n/// // … some expensive code …\n— guard drops here and we get a log line with\n//   the elapsed time\n/// ```\npub struct ScopedTimer<'a> {\n    started: Instant,\n    label: &'a str,\n}\n\nimpl<'a> ScopedTimer<'a> {\n    /// Begin measuring elapsed time for the supplied label.\n    #[inline]\n    pub fn new(label: &'a str) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            started: Instant::now(),\n            label,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Stop the timer and return the [`Duration`] since creation without\n    /// logging. This allows manual control when automatic logging at `drop`\n    /// is not desired.\n    #[inline]\n    pub fn stop(self) -> Duration {\n        Instant::now() - self.started\n    }\n}\n\nimpl<'a> Drop for ScopedTimer<'a> {\n    fn drop(&mut self) {\n        let elapsed = Instant::now() - self.started;\n        info!(\n            label = %self.label,\n            ms = %elapsed.as_millis(),\n            \"ScopedTimer finished\"\n        );\n        METRICS\n            .write()\n            .record_duration(self.label, elapsed.as_secs_f64());\n    }\n}\n\n/// Global metrics collector with *zero* external dependencies.  This is\n/// intentionally minimal because higher layers might choose to forward these\n/// numbers to Prometheus, StatsD, or another monitoring backend.\n///\n/// Internally it stores *exponential moving averages* so that it can be used\n/// in hot-paths without unbounded memory growth.\npub struct Metrics {\n    /// Moving average of durations (seconds) per label.\n    durations: std::collections::HashMap<String, f64>,\n    /// EWMA smoothing factor in `[0,1]`.\n    alpha: f64,\n}\n\nimpl Metrics {\n    fn new(alpha: f64) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            durations: std::collections::HashMap::new(),\n            alpha,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Record a latency sample under `label`.\n    pub fn record_duration(&mut self, label: impl Into<String>, secs: f64) {\n        let label = label.into();\n        let entry = self.durations.entry(label).or_insert(secs);\n        // EWMA: v_t = alpha * x_t + (1-alpha) * v_{t-1}\n        *entry = self.alpha * secs + (1.0 - self.alpha) * *entry;\n    }\n\n    /// Return the current EWMA for `label`, if any.\n    pub fn duration_avg(&self, label: &str) -> Option<f64> {\n        self.durations.get(label).copied()\n    }\n}\n\n/// Static metrics instance; tweak the smoothing factor as needed.\n/// We protect it with a *fair* RwLock to allow many concurrent readers.\nstatic METRICS: Lazy<RwLock<Metrics>> = Lazy::new(|| RwLock::new(Metrics::new(0.20)));\n\n/// Typed representation of the global **application config**.\n///\n/// This is intentionally limited to fields that the utility layer needs\n/// to know about; higher layers maintain their own extensions.\n///\n/// Because config files are versioned in the Model Registry, we include a\n/// `schema_version` field to prevent silent breakage when the expected\n/// structure changes.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct AppConfig {\n    pub schema_version: u16,\n    pub experiment_tracking_uri: Option<String>,\n    pub model_registry_uri: Option<String>,\n    pub default_retry: RetryConfig,\n    /// Additional arbitrary key/value flavour that can be stored alongside\n    /// the canonical config.  Can be abused for feature flags, etc.\n    #[serde(default)]\n    pub user: std::collections::HashMap<String, serde_json::Value>,\n}\n\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct RetryConfig {\n    pub max_attempts: usize,\n    pub backoff_millis: u64,\n}\n\nimpl Default for RetryConfig {\n    fn default() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            max_attempts: 3,\n            backoff_millis: 500,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Maximum size (bytes) that we allow a configuration file to have before\n/// we refuse to load it.  Protects against accidental loading of huge blobs.\nconst MAX_CONFIG_SIZE: usize = 256 * 1024; // 256 KiB\n\n/// Load and parse application configuration **once** at start-up.\n///\n/// The file format is auto-detected based on extension (`.yaml`/`.yml` or\n/// `.json`).  The resulting struct is cached in a global `Lazy`.\n///\n/// Example:\n/// ```no_run\n/// let cfg = visu_tility_utils::config();\n/// println!(\"Schema v{} loaded\", cfg.schema_version);\n/// ```\nfn read_config_from_disk(path: &Path) -> Result<AppConfig> {\n    if !path.exists() {\n        return Err(UtilError::ConfigNotFound(path.into()).into());\n    }\n\n    let mut file =\n        File::open(path).with_context(|| format!(\"opening configuration file `{}`\", path.display()))?;\n\n    let mut buf = Vec::new();\n    file.read_to_end(&mut buf)\n        .with_context(|| format!(\"reading configuration file `{}`\", path.display()))?;\n\n    if buf.len() > MAX_CONFIG_SIZE {\n        return Err(anyhow!(\n            \"configuration file `{}` larger than {} KiB\",\n            path.display(),\n            MAX_CONFIG_SIZE / 1024\n        ));\n    }\n\n    let cfg = match path.extension().and_then(|s| s.to_str()) {\n        Some(\"yaml\") | Some(\"yml\") => serde_yaml::from_slice(&buf)?,\n        Some(\"json\") => serde_json::from_slice(&buf)?,\n        other => {\n            return Err(anyhow!(\n                \"unsupported configuration extension {:?} for `{}`\",\n                other,\n                path.display()\n            ))\n        }\n    };\n\n    Ok(cfg)\n}\n\n/// Discover the config path in the following order:\n///\n/// 1. Explicit argument via `VISU_CONFIG` environment variable.\n/// 2. `${CWD}/visu_config.yaml`\n/// 3. `${CWD}/visu_config.json`\nfn auto_discover_config() -> Option<PathBuf> {\n    if let Ok(path) = std::env::var(\"VISU_CONFIG\") {\n        return Some(PathBuf::from(path));\n    }\n\n    const CANDIDATES: &[&str] = &[\"visu_config.yaml\", \"visu_config.yml\", \"visu_config.json\"];\n    for c in CANDIDATES {\n        let p = PathBuf::from(c);\n        if p.exists() {\n            return Some(p);\n        }\n    }\n    None\n}\n\n/// Global application configuration singleton.\nstatic CONFIG: Lazy<AppConfig> = Lazy::new(|| {\n    let path = auto_discover_config()\n        .unwrap_or_else(|| PathBuf::from(\"visu_config.yaml\")); // fallback for nicer error msg\n    match read_config_from_disk(&path) {\n        Ok(cfg) => {\n            info!(\n                file = %path.display(),\n                version = cfg.schema_version,\n                \"configuration file loaded\"\n            );\n            cfg\n        }\n        Err(e) => {\n            error!(\n                file = %path.display(),\n                error = %e,\n                \"failed to load configuration; using built-in defaults\"\n            );\n            // Fallback\n            AppConfig {\n                schema_version: 0,\n                experiment_tracking_uri: None,\n                model_registry_uri: None,\n                default_retry: RetryConfig::default(),\n                user: Default::default(),\n            }\n        }\n    }\n});\n\n/// Access the application-wide [`AppConfig`].\n#[inline]\npub fn config() -> &'static AppConfig {\n    &CONFIG\n}\n\n/// Generic exponential *back-off* / *retry* helper.\n///\n/// The provided closure is executed until it either succeeds or\n/// max_attempts have been reached.\n///\n/// Usage:\n/// ```ignore\n/// use visu_tility_utils::retry;\n///\n/// let result = retry(\n///     &config().default_retry,\n///     || {\n///         // call external dependency that might fail\n///         some_unstable_operation()\n///     });\n/// ```\npub fn retry<F, T, E>(cfg: &RetryConfig, mut f: F) -> Result<T, E>\nwhere\n    F: FnMut() -> Result<T, E>,\n{\n    let mut attempt = 0;\n    loop {\n        match f() {\n            Ok(v) => return Ok(v),\n            Err(e) if attempt + 1 == cfg.max_attempts => return Err(e),\n            Err(_e) => {\n                attempt += 1;\n                std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(cfg.backoff_millis));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Simple **ID generator** that hands out monotonically increasing\n/// identifiers from a global counter.\n///\n/// This is useful when datasets or intermediate artefacts need a\n/// transaction-like handle but heavyweight UUIDs are overkill.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]\npub struct Id(u64);\n\n// global counter\nstatic COUNTER: AtomicUsize = AtomicUsize::new(1);\n\nimpl Id {\n    /// Allocate a fresh id.\n    #[inline]\n    pub fn fresh() -> Self {\n        let n = COUNTER.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        Id(n as u64)\n    }\n\n    /// Return the underlying integer.\n    #[inline]\n    pub fn as_u64(self) -> u64 {\n        self.0\n    }\n}\n\nimpl fmt::Display for Id {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        self.0.fmt(f)\n    }\n}\n\n/// **Observer pattern**: runtime event bus.\n//\n/// Very light-weight publisher/subscriber abstraction built on top of\n/// [`crossbeam_channel`].  Since layers of the Orchestrator run in\n/// different scheduling contexts we cannot rely on *any* runtime; a\n/// channel-based design has zero dependencies on async executors.\npub mod event_bus {\n    use super::*;\n    use crossbeam_channel::{unbounded, Receiver, Sender};\n\n    /// Event types that travel on the bus.  This enum grows over time as new\n    /// features make use of the bus; by keeping it located in a *utility*\n    /// crate we avoid circular dependencies.\n    #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n    pub enum Event {\n        HyperparameterTuningStarted { experiment_id: Id },\n        HyperparameterTuningFinished { experiment_id: Id },\n        ModelRegistered {\n            name: String,\n            version: String,\n            metadata: std::collections::HashMap<String, String>,\n        },\n        ModelDriftDetected {\n            model: String,\n            magnitude: f32,\n            timestamp: chrono::DateTime<chrono::Utc>,\n        },\n        Custom(String, serde_json::Value),\n    }\n\n    /// Handle required for publishing events.\n    #[derive(Clone)]\n    pub struct Publisher {\n        tx: Sender<Event>,\n    }\n\n    impl Publisher {\n        /// Publish an event; errors are logged but otherwise ignored since the\n        /// system should remain functional even when no-one is listening.\n        pub fn publish(&self, evt: Event) {\n            if let Err(e) = self.tx.send(evt.clone()) {\n                debug!(error = ?e, event = ?evt, \"failed to publish event\");\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// A stream of events.\n    pub type Subscription = Receiver<Event>;\n\n    /// Construct a **single** global bus so that layers can obtain\n    /// cheap clones of the `Publisher` without having to pass them around.\n    static BUS: Lazy<(Publisher, Subscription)> = Lazy::new(|| {\n        let (tx, rx) = unbounded::<Event>();\n        (Publisher { tx }, rx)\n    });\n\n    /// Return a handle for *publishing* events.\n    pub fn publisher() -> Publisher {\n        BUS.0.clone()\n    }\n\n    /// Subscribe to events.  Every caller receives a *clone* of the receiver\n    /// which means all subscribers see *all* events (fan-out).\n    pub fn subscribe() -> Subscription {\n        BUS.1.clone()\n    }\n}\n\n/// A small proc-macro-like helper (implemented as declarative macro to avoid meta\n/// crate) for scoped timing.\n///\n/// ```\n/// visu_tility_utils::scope_time!(\"fancy_stage\", {\n///     expensive();\n/// });\n/// ```\n#[macro_export]\nmacro_rules! scope_time {\n    ($label:expr, $block:block) => {{\n        let _g = $crate::ScopedTimer::new($label);\n        $block\n    }};\n}\n\n/// Tests live inside the same file so that the utils crate remains\n/// a **single** compilation unit.  This is important for global singletons\n/// (`Lazy`) which must not be duplicated across multiple crates.\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn id_monotonicity() {\n        let a = Id::fresh();\n        let b = Id::fresh();\n        assert!(b.as_u64() > a.as_u64());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn retry_succeeds_eventually() {\n        let cfg = RetryConfig {\n            max_attempts: 5,\n            backoff_millis: 1,\n        };\n        let mut n = 0;\n        let res: Result<usize, usize> = retry(&cfg, || {\n            if n < 3 {\n                n += 1;\n                Err(n)\n            } else {\n                Ok(n)\n            }\n        });\n        assert_eq!(res.unwrap(), 3);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn metrics_ewma() {\n        METRICS.write().record_duration(\"foo\", 1.0);\n        METRICS.write().record_duration(\"foo\", 1.0);\n        let avg = METRICS.read().duration_avg(\"foo\").unwrap();\n        assert!(avg > 0.0 && avg <= 1.0);\n    }\n}","numLines":466,"startLine":1,"totalLines":466}}}

The measurement is therefore *scoped*; as soon as the guard\n/// goes out of scope, latency is emitted through the `tracing` facade.\n///\n/// ```no_run\n/// use visu_tility_utils::ScopedTimer;\n/// let _g = ScopedTimer::new(\"heavy_initialisation\");\n/// // … some expensive code …\n— guard drops here and we get a log line with\n//   the elapsed time\n/// ```\npub struct ScopedTimer<'a> {\n    started: Instant,\n    label: &'a str,\n}\n\nimpl<'a> ScopedTimer<'a> {\n    /// Begin measuring elapsed time for the supplied label.\n    #[inline]\n    pub fn new(label: &'a str) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            started: Instant::now(),\n            label,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Stop the timer and return the [`Duration`] since creation without\n    /// logging. This allows manual control when automatic logging at `drop`\n    /// is not desired.\n    #[inline]\n    pub fn stop(self) -> Duration {\n        Instant::now() - self.started\n    }\n}\n\nimpl<'a> Drop for ScopedTimer<'a> {\n    fn drop(&mut self) {\n        let elapsed = Instant::now() - self.started;\n        info!(\n            label = %self.label,\n            ms = %elapsed.as_millis(),\n            \"ScopedTimer finished\"\n        );\n        METRICS\n            .write()\n            .record_duration(self.label, elapsed.as_secs_f64());\n    }\n}\n\n/// Global metrics collector with *zero* external dependencies.  This is\n/// intentionally minimal because higher layers might choose to forward these\n/// numbers to Prometheus, StatsD, or another monitoring backend.\n///\n/// Internally it stores *exponential moving averages* so that it can be used\n/// in hot-paths without unbounded memory growth.\npub struct Metrics {\n    /// Moving average of durations (seconds) per label.\n    durations: std::collections::HashMap<String, f64>,\n    /// EWMA smoothing factor in `[0,1]`.\n    alpha: f64,\n}\n\nimpl Metrics {\n    fn new(alpha: f64) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            durations: std::collections::HashMap::new(),\n            alpha,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Record a latency sample under `label`.\n    pub fn record_duration(&mut self, label: impl Into<String>, secs: f64) {\n        let label = label.into();\n        let entry = self.durations.entry(label).or_insert(secs);\n        // EWMA: v_t = alpha * x_t + (1-alpha) * v_{t-1}\n        *entry = self.alpha * secs + (1.0 - self.alpha) * *entry;\n    }\n\n    /// Return the current EWMA for `label`, if any.\n    pub fn duration_avg(&self, label: &str) -> Option<f64> {\n        self.durations.get(label).copied()\n    }\n}\n\n/// Static metrics instance; tweak the smoothing factor as needed.\n/// We protect it with a *fair* RwLock to allow many concurrent readers.\nstatic METRICS: Lazy<RwLock<Metrics>> = Lazy::new(|| RwLock::new(Metrics::new(0.20)));\n\n/// Typed representation of the global **application config**.\n///\n/// This is intentionally limited to fields that the utility layer needs\n/// to know about; Protects against accidental loading of huge blobs.\nconst MAX_CONFIG_SIZE: usize = 256 * 1024; // 256 KiB\n\n/// Load and parse application configuration **once** at start-up.\n///\n/// The file format is auto-detected based on extension (`.yaml`/`.yml` or\n/// `.json`).  The resulting struct is cached in a global `Lazy`.\n///\n/// Example:\n/// ```no_run\n/// let cfg = visu_tility_utils::config();\n/// println!(\"Schema v{} loaded\", cfg.schema_version);\n/// ```\nfn read_config_from_disk(path: &Path) -> Result<AppConfig> {\n    if !path.exists() {\n        return Err(UtilError::ConfigNotFound(path.into()).into());\n    }\n\n    let mut file =\n        File::open(path).with_context(|| format!(\"opening configuration file `{}`\", path.display()))?;\n\n    let mut buf = Vec::new();\n    file.read_to_end(&mut buf)\n        .with_context(|| format!(\"reading configuration file `{}`\", path.display()))?;\n\n    if buf.len() > MAX_CONFIG_SIZE {\n        return Err(anyhow!(\n            \"configuration file `{}` larger than {} KiB\",\n            path.display(),\n            MAX_CONFIG_SIZE / 1024\n        ));\n    }\n\n    let cfg = match path.extension().and_then(|s| s.to_str()) {\n        Some(\"yaml\") | Some(\"yml\") => serde_yaml::from_slice(&buf)?,\n        Some(\"json\") => serde_json::from_slice(&buf)?,\n        other => {\n            return Err(anyhow!(\n                \"unsupported configuration extension {:?} for `{}`\",\n                other,\n                path.display()\n            ))\n        }\n    };\n\n    Ok(cfg)\n}\n\n/// Discover the config path in the following order:\n///\n/// 1. std::env::var(\"VISU_CONFIG\") {\n        return Some(PathBuf::from(path));\n    }\n\n    const CANDIDATES: &[&str] = &[\"visu_config.yaml\", \"visu_config.yml\", \"visu_config.json\"];\n    for c in CANDIDATES {\n        let p = PathBuf::from(c);\n        if p.exists() {\n            return Some(p);\n        }\n    }\n    None\n}\n\n/// Global application configuration singleton.\nstatic CONFIG: until it either succeeds or\n/// max_attempts have been reached.\n///\n/// Usage:\n/// ```ignore\n/// use visu_tility_utils::retry;\n///\n/// let result = retry(\n///     &config().default_retry,\n///     || {\n///         // call external dependency that might fail\n///         some_unstable_operation()\n///     });\n/// ```\npub fn retry<F, T, E>(cfg: &RetryConfig, mut f: F) -> Result<T, E>\nwhere\n    F: FnMut() -> Result<T, E>,\n{\n    let mut attempt = 0;\n    loop {\n        match f() {\n            Ok(v) => return Ok(v),\n            Err(e) if attempt + 1 == cfg.max_attempts => return Err(e),\n            Err(_e) => {\n                attempt += 1;\n                std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(cfg.backoff_millis));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Simple **ID generator** that hands out monotonically increasing\n/// identifiers from a global counter.\n///\n/// This is useful when datasets or intermediate artefacts need a\n/// transaction-like handle but heavyweight UUIDs are overkill.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]\npub struct Id(u64);\n\n// global counter\nstatic COUNTER: AtomicUsize = AtomicUsize::new(1);\n\nimpl Id {\n    /// Allocate a fresh id.\n    #[inline]\n    pub fn fresh() -> Self {\n        let n = COUNTER.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        Id(n as u64)\n    }\n\n    /// Return the underlying integer.\n    #[inline]\n    pub fn as_u64(self) -> u64 {\n        self.0\n    }\n}\n\nimpl fmt::Display for Id {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        self.0.fmt(f)\n    }\n}\n\n/// **Observer pattern**: runtime event bus.\n//\n/// Very light-weight publisher/subscriber abstraction built on top of\n/// [`crossbeam_channel`].  Since layers of the Orchestrator run in\n/// different scheduling contexts we cannot rely on *any* runtime; a\n/// channel-based design has zero dependencies on async executors.\npub mod event_bus {\n    use super::*;\n    use crossbeam_channel::{unbounded, Receiver, Sender};\n\n    /// Event types that travel on the bus.  This enum grows over time as new\n    /// features make use of the bus; by keeping it located in a *utility*\n    /// crate we avoid circular dependencies.\n    #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n    pub enum Event {\n        HyperparameterTuningStarted { experiment_id: Id },\n        HyperparameterTuningFinished { experiment_id: Id },\n        ModelRegistered {\n            name: String,\n            version: String,\n            metadata: std::collections::HashMap<String, String>,\n        },\n        ModelDriftDetected {\n            model: String,\n            magnitude: f32,\n            timestamp: chrono::DateTime<chrono::Utc>,\n        },\n        Custom(String, serde_json::Value),\n    }\n\n    /// Handle required for publishing events.\n    #[derive(Clone)]\n    pub struct Publisher {\n        tx: Sender<Event>,\n    }\n\n    impl Publisher {\n        /// Publish an event; errors are logged but otherwise ignored since the\n        /// system should remain functional even when no-one is listening.\n        pub fn publish(&self, evt: Event) {\n            if let Err(e) = self.tx.send(evt.clone()) {\n                debug!(error = ?e, event = ?evt, \"failed to publish event\");\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// A stream of events.\n    pub type Subscription = Receiver<Event>;\n\n    /// Construct a **single** global bus so that layers can obtain\n    /// cheap clones of the `Publisher` without having to pass them around.\n    static BUS: Lazy<(Publisher, Subscription)> = Lazy::new(|| {\n        let (tx, rx) = unbounded::<Event>();\n        (Publisher { tx }, rx)\n    });\n\n    /// Return a handle for *publishing* events.\n    pub fn publisher() -> Publisher {\n        BUS.0.clone()\n    }\n\n    /// Subscribe to events.  Every caller receives a *clone* of the receiver\n    /// which means all subscribers see *all* events (fan-out).\n    pub fn subscribe() -> Subscription {\n        BUS.1.clone()\n    }\n}\n\n/// A small proc-macro-like helper (implemented as declarative macro to avoid meta\n/// crate) for scoped timing.\n///\n/// ```\n/// visu_tility_utils::scope_time!(\"fancy_stage\", {\n///     expensive();\n/// });\n/// ```\n#[macro_export]\nmacro_rules! scope_time {\n    ($label:expr, $block:block) => {{\n        let _g = $crate::ScopedTimer::new($label);\n        $block\n    }};\n}\n\n/// module.\n/// It provides direct integration with the underlying metrics infrastructure.\npub(crate) struct RawMetricClient {\n    endpoint: String,\n}\n\nimpl RawMetricClient {\n    /// Create a new metric client pointing to the specified endpoint.\n    pub(crate) fn new(endpoint: impl Into<String>) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            endpoint: endpoint.into(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Log metric = %metric_name,\n            value = %value,\n            step = %step,\n            \"RawMetricClient: logged scalar\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    /// Log a hyperparameter configuration.\n    pub(crate) fn log_hyperparameter(&self, param_name: &str, value: &str) {\n        info!(\n            endpoint = %self.endpoint,\n            param = %param_name,\n            value = %value,\n            \"RawMetricClient: logged hyperparameter\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    /// Upload a model artifact to the tracking server.\n    pub(crate) fn upload_model_artifact(\n        &self,\n }\n}\n\n/// Tests live inside the same file so that the utils crate remains\n/// a **single** compilation unit. The resulting struct is cached in a global `Lazy`.\n///\n/// Example:\n/// ```no_run\n/// let cfg = visu_tility_utils::config();\n/// println!(\"Schema v{} loaded\", cfg.schema_version);\n/// ```\nfn read_config_from_disk(path: &Path) -> Result<AppConfig> {\n    if !path.exists() {\n        return Err(UtilError::ConfigNotFound(path.into()).into());\n    }\n\n    let mut file =\n        File::open(path).with_context(|| format!(\"opening configuration file `{}`\", path.display()))?;\n\n    let mut buf = Vec::new();\n    file.read_to_end(&mut buf)\n        .with_context(|| format!(\"reading configuration file `{}`\", path.display()))?;\n\n    if buf.len() > MAX_CONFIG_SIZE {\n        return Err(anyhow!(\n            \"configuration file `{}` larger than {} KiB\",\n            path.display(),\n            MAX_CONFIG_SIZE / 1024\n        ));\n    }\n\n    let cfg = match path.extension().and_then(|s| s.to_str()) {\n        Some(\"yaml\") | Some(\"yml\") => serde_yaml::from_slice(&buf)?,\n        Some(\"json\") => serde_json::from_slice(&buf)?,\n        other => {\n            return Err(anyhow!(\n                \"unsupported configuration extension {:?} for `{}`\",\n                other,\n                path.display()\n            ))\n        }\n    };\n\n    Ok(cfg)\n}\n\n/// Discover the config path in the following order:\n///\n/// 1. Explicit argument via `VISU_CONFIG` environment variable.\n/// 2. `${CWD}/visu_config.yaml`\n/// 3. `${CWD}/visu_config.json`\nfn auto_discover_config() -> Option<PathBuf> {\n    if let Ok(path) = std::env::var(\"VISU_CONFIG\") {\n        return Some(PathBuf::from(path));\n    }\n\n    const CANDIDATES: &[&str] = &[\"visu_config.yaml\", \"visu_config.yml\", \"visu_config.json\"];\n    for c in CANDIDATES {\n        let p = PathBuf::from(c);\n        if p.exists() {\n            return Some(p);\n        }\n    }\n    None\n}\n\n/// Global application configuration singleton.\nstatic CONFIG: Lazy<AppConfig> = Lazy::new(|| {\n    let path = auto_discover_config()\n        .unwrap_or_else(|| PathBuf::from(\"visu_config.yaml\")); // fallback for nicer error msg\n    match read_config_from_disk(&path) {\n        Ok(cfg) => {\n            info!(\n                file = %path.display(),\n                version = cfg.schema_version,\n                \"configuration file loaded\"\n            );\n            cfg\n        }\n        Err(e) => {\n            error!(\n                file = %path.display(),\n                error = %e,\n                \"failed to load configuration; using built-in defaults\"\n            );\n            // Fallback\n            AppConfig {\n                schema_version: 0,\n                experiment_tracking_uri: None,\n                model_registry_uri: None,\n                default_retry: RetryConfig::default(),\n                user: Default::default(),\n            }\n        }\n    }\n});\n\n/// Access the application-wide [`AppConfig`].\n#[inline]\npub fn config() -> &'static AppConfig {\n    &CONFIG\n}\n\n/// Generic exponential *back-off* / *retry* helper.\n///\n/// The provided closure is executed until it either succeeds or\n/// max_attempts have been reached.\n///\n/// Usage:\n/// ```ignore\n/// use visu_tility_utils::retry;\n///\n/// let result = retry(\n///     &config().default_retry,\n///     || {\n///         // call external dependency that might fail\n///         some_unstable_operation()\n///     });\n/// ```\npub fn retry<F, T, E>(cfg: &RetryConfig, mut f: F) -> Result<T, E>\nwhere\n    F: FnMut() -> Result<T, E>,\n{\n    let mut attempt = 0;\n    loop {\n        match f() {\n            Ok(v) => return Ok(v),\n            Err(e) if attempt + 1 == cfg.max_attempts => return Err(e),\n            Err(_e) => {\n                attempt += 1;\n                std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(cfg.backoff_millis));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Simple **ID generator** that hands out monotonically increasing\n/// identifiers from a global counter.\n///\n/// This is useful when datasets or intermediate artefacts need a\n/// transaction-like handle but heavyweight UUIDs are overkill.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]\npub struct Id(u64);\n\n// global counter\nstatic COUNTER: AtomicUsize = AtomicUsize::new(1);\n\nimpl Id {\n    /// Allocate a fresh id.\n    #[inline]\n    pub fn fresh() -> Self {\n        let n = COUNTER.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        Id(n as u64)\n    }\n\n    /// Return the underlying integer.\n    #[inline]\n    pub fn as_u64(self) -> u64 {\n        self.0\n    }\n}\n\nimpl fmt::Display for Id {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        self.0.fmt(f)\n    }\n}\n\n/// **Observer pattern**: runtime event bus.\n//\n/// Very light-weight publisher/subscriber abstraction built on top of\n/// [`crossbeam_channel`].  Since layers of the Orchestrator run in\n/// different scheduling contexts we cannot rely on *any* runtime; a\n/// channel-based design has zero dependencies on async executors.\npub mod event_bus {\n    use super::*;\n    use crossbeam_channel::{unbounded, Receiver, Sender};\n\n    /// Event types that travel on the bus.  This enum grows over time as new\n    /// features make use of the bus; by keeping it located in a *utility*\n    /// crate we avoid circular dependencies.\n    #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n    pub enum Event {\n        HyperparameterTuningStarted { experiment_id: Id },\n        HyperparameterTuningFinished { experiment_id: Id },\n        ModelRegistered {\n            name: String,\n            version: String,\n            metadata: std::collections::HashMap<String, String>,\n        },\n        ModelDriftDetected {\n            model: String,\n            magnitude: f32,\n            timestamp: chrono::DateTime<chrono::Utc>,\n        },\n        Custom(String, serde_json::Value),\n    }\n\n    /// Handle required for publishing events.\n    #[derive(Clone)]\n    pub struct Publisher {\n        tx: Sender<Event>,\n    }\n\n    impl Publisher {\n        /// Publish an event; errors are logged but otherwise ignored since the\n        /// system should remain functional even when no-one is listening.\n        pub fn publish(&self, evt: Event) {\n            if let Err(e) = self.tx.send(evt.clone()) {\n                debug!(error = ?e, event = ?evt, \"failed to publish event\");\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// A stream of events.\n    pub type Subscription = Receiver<Event>;\n\n    /// Construct a **single** global bus so that layers can obtain\n    /// cheap clones of the `Publisher` without having to pass them around.\n    static BUS: Lazy<(Publisher, Subscription)> = Lazy::new(|| {\n        let (tx, rx) = unbounded::<Event>();\n        (Publisher { tx }, rx)\n    });\n\n    /// Return a handle for *publishing* events.\n    pub fn publisher() -> Publisher {\n        BUS.0.clone()\n    }\n\n    /// Subscribe to events.  Every caller receives a *clone* of the receiver\n    /// which means all subscribers see *all* events (fan-out).\n    pub fn subscribe() -> Subscription {\n        BUS.1.clone()\n    }\n}\n\n/// A small proc-macro-like helper (implemented as declarative macro to avoid meta\n/// crate) for scoped timing.\n///\n/// ```\n/// visu_tility_utils::scope_time!(\"fancy_stage\", {\n///     expensive();\n/// });\n/// ```\n#[macro_export]\nmacro_rules! scope_time {\n    ($label:expr, $block:block) => {{\n        let _g = $crate::ScopedTimer::new($label);\n        $block\n    }};\n}\n\n/// Low-level metric client for experiment tracking.\n///\n/// This struct is crate-private and should only be accessed through the\n/// `ExperimentTracker` trait abstraction in the `experiment_tracker` module.\n/// It provides direct integration with the underlying metrics infrastructure.\npub(crate) struct RawMetricClient {\n    endpoint: String,\n}\n\nimpl RawMetricClient {\n    /// Create a new metric client pointing to the specified endpoint.\n    pub(crate) fn new(endpoint: impl Into<String>) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            endpoint: endpoint.into(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Log a scalar metric value at a given training step.\n    pub(crate) fn log_scalar(&self, metric_name: &str, value: f64, step: u64) {\n        info!(\n            endpoint = %self.endpoint,\n            metric = %metric_name,\n            value = %value,\n            step = %step,\n            \"RawMetricClient: logged scalar\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    /// Log a hyperparameter configuration.\n     \"RawMetricClient: logged hyperparameter\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    /// Upload a model artifact to the tracking server.\n    pub(crate) fn upload_model_artifact(\n        &self,\n    remote_name = %remote_name,\n            \"RawMetricClient: uploaded model artifact\"\n        );\n        // In a real implementation, this would perform actual file upload\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/// Tests live inside the same file so that the utils crate remains\n/// a **single** compilation unit. Tuning strategies (`GridSearchTuner`,\n//! `RandomSearchTuner`, …) implement the [`HyperParamTuner`] trait,\n//! while interested parties subscribe to an async broadcast bus to\n//! receive fine-grained tuning events in real time.\n//!\n//! The code is self-contained and can be compiled independently, yet\n//! integrates cleanly into the VisuTility Orchestrator ecosystem:\n//!   • Uses `async_trait` for async polymorphism\n//!   • Emits structured logs via `tracing`\n//!   • Relies on `serde_json::Value` for schema-less parameter configs\n//!   • Provides unit tests powered by `tokio` runtime\n//!   • Uses `ExperimentTracker` trait for decoupled experiment tracking\n//!\n//! MetricDirection,\n}\n\nimpl Metric {\n    /// Returns `true` if `self` is better than `other` w.r.t. the\n    /// optimization direction.\n    pub fn is_better_than(&self, other: &Self) -> bool {\n        match self.direction {\n            MetricDirection::Maximize => self.value > other.value,\n            MetricDirection::Minimize => self.value < other.value,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Optimization direction for a metric.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub enum MetricDirection {\n    Maximize,\n    Minimize,\n}\n\nimpl Display for MetricDirection {\n    fn Domain-specific errors during tuning.\n#[derive(Error, Debug)]\npub enum TuningError {\n    #[error(\"model training failed: {0}\")]\n    TrainingFailure(String),\n\n    #[error(\"evaluation timed out\")]\n    EvalTimeout,\n\n    #[error(\"search space exhausted without receiving any metric\")]\n    EmptySearchSpace,\n\n    #[error(\"internal error: {0}\")]\n    Internal(String),\n}\n\nimpl   TrialComplete {\n        trial_id: usize,\n        params: HyperParams,\n        metric: Result<Metric, TuningError>,\n    },\n    TuningComplete {\n        result: Result<TuningResult,  pub fn new(buffer: usize) -> Self {\n        let (tx, _) = broadcast::channel(buffer);\n        Self { tx }\n    }\n\n    pub fn subscribe(&self) -> broadcast::Receiver<TuningEvent> {\n        self.tx.subscribe()\n    }\n\n    pub fn emit(&self, e: TuningEvent) {\n        // Ignoring send errors (occurs if no receiver is listening).\n        let _ = self.tx.send(e);\n    }\n}\n\n/// Trait }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl<T> HyperParamTuner for GridSearchTuner<T>\nwhere\n    T: ModelTrainer + 'static,\n{\n    #[instrument(skip_all, name = \"grid_search_tune\")]\n    async fn tune(&self) -> best_metric: Option<Metric> = None;\n        let mut best_params: Option<HyperParams> = None;\n\n        let mut trials = stream::iter(self.search_space.clone().into_iter().enumerate());\n\n        while let Some((trial_id, params)) = trials.next().await {\n            ctx.event_bus.emit(TuningEvent::TrialStart {\n                trial_id,\n                params: params.clone(),\n            });\n\n            let metric_res = match ctx.max_duration {\n                Some(dur) => match timeout(dur, ctx.trainer.train_and_eval(&params)).await {\n                    Ok(res) => res,\n                    Err(_) => Err(TuningError::EvalTimeout),\n                },\n                None => ctx.trainer.train_and_eval(&params).await,\n            };\n\n &metric_res {\n                Ok(metric) => {\n                    let better = best_metric\n                        .as_ref()\n                        .map(|m| metric.is_better_than(m))\n                        .unwrap_or(true);\n                    if better {\n                        best_metric = Some(metric.clone());\n                        best_params = Some(params);\n                    }\n                }\n                Err(e) => warn!(trial_id, error = %e, \"grid-search trial failed\"),\n            }\n        }\n\n        let result = match (best_params, best_metric) {\n Self {\n            context: TunerContext {\n                trainer,\n                event_bus: TuningEventBus::new(event_buffer),\n                max_duration,\n            },\n params_pool.truncate(self.max_trials);\n\n        let mut best_metric: Option<Metric> = None;\n        let mut best_params: Option<HyperParams> = None;\n\n        for (trial_id, params) in               trial_id,\n                params: params.clone(),\n                metric: metric_res.clone(),\n            });\n\n            match &metric_res {\n                Ok(metric) => {\n if better {\n                        best_metric = Some(metric.clone());\n                        best_params = Some(params);\n                    }\n                }\n                Err(e) => {\n                best_params: p,\n                best_metric: m,\n            }),\n            _ => Err(TuningError::TrainingFailure(\n                \"all random-search trials failed\".into(),\n            )),\n        };\n\n        ctx.event_bus\n            .emit(TuningEvent::TuningComplete { result: result.clone() });\n\n        result\n    }\n}\n\n//\n// ───────────────────────────────\n// usize, params: &HyperParams) {\n        debug!(trial_id, params = %serde_json::to_string(params).unwrap_or_default(), \"trial started\");\n    }\n\n    #[instrument(skip_all)]\n    async fn }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Attach an observer to the bus; returns a detach-able async task.\npub fn attach_observer<O: TuningEventObserver + 'static>(\n    bus: &TuningEventBus,\n    observer: Arc<O>,\n) -> tokio::task::JoinHandle<()> {\n    let mut rx = bus.subscribe();\n    tokio::spawn(async move {\n        while let Ok(event) = rx.recv().await {\n            match event {\n                TuningEvent::TrialStart { trial_id, params } => {\n                    observer.on_trial_start(trial_id, &params).await;\n                }\n                TuningEvent::TrialComplete {\n It accepts any type that implements `ExperimentTracker`, allowing for\n/// easy testing with mocks and swapping of tracking backends.\npub fn log_hyperparameters_to_tracker<T: ExperimentTracker>(\n    tracker: &T,\n    params: &HyperParams,\n) {\n    for (name, value) in params {\n        tracker.log_hyperparameter(name, &value.to_string());\n    }\n}\n\n/// Log tuning metrics using the Hyper-parameter Tuning, Model-Monitoring and Automated-Retraining\n//! ================================================================\n//! This module glues together three critical concerns of the VisuTility\n//! Orchestrator platform:\n//!\n//! 1. Strategy Pattern‐based hyper-parameter tuning\n//! 2. Observer Pattern‐based model monitoring\n//! 3. Automated retraining triggers\n//!\n//! The code is completely self-contained and can be compiled and unit-tested\n//! in isolation.  In the full platform the public interfaces defined here\n//! are re-exported by the *Model Ops* layer.\n//!\n//! This module uses the `ExperimentTracker` trait abstraction for all\n//! experiment tracking operations, enabling easy swapping of backends\n//! and mock-based testing.\n//!\n//! Dependencies\n//! ------------\n//! The following third-party crates are required in `Cargo.toml`.\n//!\n//! ```toml\n//! [dependencies]\n//! async-trait = \"0.1\"\n//! rand        = \"0.8\"\n//! thiserror   = \"1.0\"\n//! tokio       = { version = \"1\", features = [\"full\"] }\n//! log         = \"0.4\"\n//! ```\n//!\n//! Note: In production, all blocking‐IO should be pushed to separate worker\n//! pools; here tokio is used for concise asynchronous examples.\n\nuse std::collections::HashMap;\nuse std::path::Path;\nuse std::sync::{Arc, RwLock};\nuse std::time::{Duration, SystemTime};\n\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse log::{debug, error, info, warn};\nuse rand::distributions::{Distribution, Uniform};\nuse rand::rngs::ThreadRng;\nuse thiserror::Error;\nuse tokio::task;\nuse tokio::time::sleep;\n\nuse crate::experiment_tracker::ExperimentTracker;\n\n//\n// Domain Types\n// ------------\n\n/// A unique model identifier.\npub type ModelId = String;\n\n/// A simplistic representation of metric values keyed by their names.\npub type Metrics = HashMap<String, f64>;\n\n/// Hyper-parameter key/value store. In production this would wrap an Arrow table,\n/// Parquet reader, or a stream of image tensors.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub struct DataBatch {\n    pub num_samples: usize,\n}\n\n//\n// Error Handling\n// --------------\n\n#[derive(Debug, Error)]\npub enum ModelOpsError {\n    #[error(\"model `{0}` not found\")]\n    ModelNotFound(ModelId),\n\n    #[error(\"failed to spawn background task: {0}\")]\n    TaskSpawnError(String),\n\n    #[error(\"training failed: {0}\")]\n    TrainingFailed(String),\n\n    #[error(\"registry error: {0}\")]\n    Registry(String),\n}\n\n//\n//  Model Registry\n// ---------------\n\n/// Metadata for a single, immutable model version.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub struct ModelVersion {\n    pub version: u64,\n    pub trained_at: SystemTime,\n    pub hyper_params: HyperParams,\n    pub metrics: Metrics,\n}\n\n/// Thread-safe, in-memory implementation of a model registry.\n///\n/// In production this would be backed by a SQL/NoSQL database with audit\n/// logging. All writes are versioned and immutable.\n#[derive(Debug, Default, Clone)]\npub struct ModelRegistry {\n    inner: Arc<RwLock<HashMap<ModelId, Vec<ModelVersion>>>>,\n}\n\nimpl ModelRegistry {\n    /// Registers a new model version and returns the assigned version number.\n    pub fn register(\n        &self,\n        id: &ModelId,\n        hyper_params: HyperParams,\n        metrics: Metrics,\n    ) -> Result<u64, ModelOpsError> {\n        let mut guard = self\n            .inner\n            .write()\n            .map_err(|e| ModelOpsError::Registry(e.to_string()))?;\n\n        let versions = guard.entry(id.clone()).or_default();\n        let version_num = versions\n            .last()\n            .map(|v| v.version + 1)\n            .unwrap_or(1);\n\n        versions.push(ModelVersion {\n            version: version_num,\n            trained_at: SystemTime::now(),\n            hyper_params,\n            metrics,\n        });\n\n        info!(\"Registered model `{id}` version {version_num}\");\n        Ok(version_num)\n    }\n\n    /// Returns the latest model version.\n    pub fn latest(&self, id: &ModelId) -> Result<ModelVersion, ModelOpsError> {\n        let guard = self\n            .inner\n            .read()\n            .map_err(|e| ModelOpsError::Registry(e.to_string()))?;\n\n        guard\n            .get(id)\n            .and_then(|v| v.last().cloned())\n            .ok_or_else(|| ModelOpsError::ModelNotFound(id.clone()))\n    }\n}\n\n//\n// Strategy Pattern: Hyper-Parameter Tuners\n// ---------------------------------------\n\n/// Strategies must implement asynchronous tuning given a model id and data.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait HyperParamTuner: Send + Sync {\n    async fn tune(\n        &self,\n        model_id: &ModelId,\n        data: &DataBatch,\n    ) -> Result<HyperParams, ModelOpsError>;\n}\n\n/// A simple grid search tuner.\npub struct GridSearchTuner {\n    learning_rates: Vec<f64>,\n    num_layers: Vec<f64>,\n}\n\nimpl GridSearchTuner {\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            learning_rates: vec![1e-4, 1e-3, 1e-2],\n            num_layers: vec![2.0, 3.0, 4.0],\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl HyperParamTuner for GridSearchTuner {\n    async fn tune(\n        &self,\n        model_id: &ModelId,\n        data: &DataBatch,\n    ) -> Result<HyperParams, ModelOpsError> {\n        info!(\n            \"Running grid search for model `{}` on {} samples\",\n            model_id, data.num_samples\n        );\n\n        // Dummy evaluation metric: random value ∈ [0,1]\n        let mut best_metric = f64::MIN;\n        let mut best_params = HyperParams::new();\n\n        for lr in &self.learning_rates {\n            for layers in &self.num_layers {\n                let simulated_metric = 1.0 / (lr * layers); // deterministic but arbitrary\n                if simulated_metric > best_metric {\n                    best_metric = simulated_metric;\n                    best_params.insert(\"learning_rate\".into(), *lr);\n                    best_params.insert(\"num_layers\".into(), *layers);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        info!(\"Grid search selected params: {:?}\", best_params);\n        Ok(best_params)\n    }\n}\n\n/// A random search tuner.\npub struct RandomSearchTuner {\n    trials: usize,\n    rng: ThreadRng,\n}\n\nimpl RandomSearchTuner {\n    pub fn new(trials: usize) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            trials,\n            rng: rand::thread_rng(),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl HyperParamTuner for RandomSearchTuner {\n    async fn tune(\n        &self,\n        model_id: &ModelId,\n        data: &DataBatch,\n    ) -> Result<HyperParams, ModelOpsError> {\n        info!(\n            \"Running random search for model `{}` with {} trials\",\n            model_id, self.trials\n        );\n\n        let lr_dist = Uniform::new(1e-4, 1e-2);\n        let layer_dist = Uniform::new_inclusive(2.0, 6.0);\n\n        let mut best_metric = f64::MIN;\n        let mut best_params = HyperParams::new();\n\n        for _ in 0..self.trials {\n            let lr = lr_dist.sample(&mut self.rng);\n            let layers = layer_dist.sample(&mut self.rng);\n            let simulated_metric = 1.0 / (lr * layers);\n\n            if simulated_metric > best_metric {\n                best_metric = simulated_metric;\n                best_params.insert(\"learning_rate\".into(), lr);\n                best_params.insert(\"num_layers\".into(), layers);\n            }\n        }\n\n        info!(\"Random search selected params: {:?}\", best_params);\n        Ok(best_params)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Factory GridSearch,\n    RandomSearch { trials: usize },\n}\n\npub fn tuner_factory(kind: TunerKind) -> Arc<dyn HyperParamTuner> {\n    match kind {\n        TunerKind::GridSearch => Arc::new(GridSearchTuner::new()),\n        TunerKind::RandomSearch { trials } => Arc::new(RandomSearchTuner::new(trials)),\n    }\n}\n\n//\n// Observer Pattern: Metrics Events & Subscriptions\n// -----------------------------------------------\n\n/// A metrics event emitted by the serving layer.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub struct MetricsEvent {\n    pub model_id: ModelId,\n    pub metrics: Metrics,\n}\n\n/// Observer trait used by any consumer interested in metrics events.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait Observer: Send + Sync {\n    async fn update(&self, event: MetricsEvent);\n}\n\n/// Subject trait implemented by event producers.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait Subject {\n    async fn add_observer(&self, observer: Arc<dyn Observer>);\n    async fn notify(&self, event: MetricsEvent);\n}\n\n/// Model monitor aggregates runtime metrics and notifies observers when\n/// degradation is detected.\n#[derive(Default)]\npub struct ModelMonitor {\n    observers: Arc<RwLock<Vec<Arc<dyn Observer>>>>,\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl Subject for ModelMonitor {\n    async fn add_observer(&self, observer: Arc<dyn Observer>) {\n        let mut guard = self\n            .observers\n            .write()\n            .expect(\"poisoned lock in ModelMonitor\");\n        guard.push(observer);\n    }\n\n    async fn notify(&self, event: MetricsEvent) {\n        debug!(\"Notifying {} observers\", self.observers.read().unwrap().len());\n        for obs in self.observers.read().unwrap().iter() {\n            obs.update(event.clone()).await;\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n//\n// Experiment Tracking Integration\n// -------------------------------\n\n/// Log hyperparameters to an experiment tracker.\n///\n/// This function accepts any type that implements the {\n    for (name, value) in params {\n        tracker.log_hyperparameter(name, &value.to_string());\n    }\n}\n\n/// Log training metrics to an experiment tracker.\n///\n/// Records each metric in the provided map at the specified step.\npub fn log_training_metrics<T: ExperimentTracker>(\n    tracker: &T,\n    metrics: &Metrics,\n    step: u64,\n) {\n    for (name, value) in metrics {\n        tracker.log_scalar(name, *value, step);\n    }\n}\n\n/// Upload a model artifact using the experiment tracker.\n///\n/// Delegates to the tracker's `upload_model_artifact` method.\npub fn registry,\n            tuner,\n            degradation_threshold,\n            tracker,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Actual training routine.  In production this would call into a training\n    /// service, ship GPU jobs, etc.\n    async fn train_model(\n        &self,\n        model_id: &ModelId,\n        hyper_params: HyperParams,\n        data: DataBatch,\n    ) -> Result<Metrics, ModelOpsError> {\n        info!(\"Training model `{model_id}` with params {:?}\", hyper_params);\n\n        // Log hyperparameters to tracker\n        log_hyperparams(self.tracker.as_ref(), &hyper_params);\n\n        // Fake training latency\n        sleep(Duration::from_millis(500)).await;\n\n        // Return fake metrics inversely proportional to learning rate\n        let val_accuracy = 0.85\n            + 0.1 * (1e-2 - hyper_params[\"learning_rate\"]).clamp(0.0, 1e-2) / 1e-2;\n\n        let metrics = HashMap::from([(\"val_accuracy\".into(), val_accuracy)]);\n\n        // Log training metrics\n        log_training_metrics(self.tracker.as_ref(), &metrics, 1);\n\n        Ok(metrics)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Run a complete training pipeline with experiment tracking.\n///\n/// This function coordinates hyperparameter tuning, model training, and\n/// result logging through the provided `ExperimentTracker`.\npub async fn run_training_pipeline<T: ExperimentTracker>(\n    tracker: &T,\n    tuner: Arc<dyn HyperParamTuner>,\n    model_id: &ModelId,\n    data: DataBatch,\n    registry: &ModelRegistry,\n) -> Result<u64, ModelOpsError> {\n    // Step 1: Hyperparameter tuning\n    let params = tuner.tune(model_id, &data).await?;\n    log_hyperparams(tracker, &params);\n\n    // Step 2: Train model (simulated)\n    info!(\"Training model val_accuracy)]);\n\n    // Step 3: Log metrics\n    log_training_metrics(tracker, &metrics, 1);\n\n    // Step 4: Register model\n    let version = registry.register(model_id, params, metrics)?;\n\n    info!(\"Model `{model_id}` trained and registered as version {version}\");\n    Ok(version)\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl<T: ExperimentTracker + Send + Sync + 'static> Observer for AutoRetrainer<T> {\n    async fn update(&self, event: MetricsEvent) {\n        let acc = event.metrics.get(\"val_accuracy\").cloned().unwrap_or(1.0);\n        if acc >= self.degradation_threshold {\n            debug!(\n                \"Model `{}` accuracy {:.3} above threshold {:.3}, no action.\",\n                event.model_id, acc, self.degradation_threshold\n            );\n            return;\n        }\n\n tuned_params.clone(),\n                data,\n                tracker_clone.as_ref(),\n            ).await?;\n\n            // Step 4: Register new version\n            registry_clone\n                .register(&model_id, tuned_params, new_metrics)\n                .map(|v| info!(\"Model `{}` retrained, new version {}\", model_id, v))\n        })\n        .await\n        {\n            error!(\"Failed to spawn retraining task: {e}\");\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// A static helper so we can move it into the spawned async task easily.\nasync fn train_model_static<T: ExperimentTracker>(\n    model_id: &ModelId,\n    hyper_params: HyperParams,\n    _data: DataBatch,\n    tracker: &T,\n) -> Result<Metrics, ModelOpsError> {\n    info!(\"Training model `{model_id}` with params {:?}\", hyper_params);\n\n    // Log hyperparameters\n    log_hyperparams(tracker, &hyper_params);\n\n    // Fake training latency\n    sleep(Duration::from_millis(500)).await;\n\n    // HashMap::from([(\"val_accuracy\".into(), val_accuracy)]);\n\n    // Log training metrics\n    log_training_metrics(tracker, &metrics, 1);\n\n    Ok(metrics)\n}\n\n//\n// Example Usage\n// -------------\n//! Hyper-parameter Tuning, Model-Monitoring and Automated-Retraining\n//! ================================================================\n//! This module glues together three critical concerns of the VisuTility\n//! Orchestrator platform:\n//!\n//! 1. Strategy Pattern‐based hyper-parameter tuning\n//! 2. Observer Pattern‐based model monitoring\n//! 3. Automated retraining triggers\n//!\n//! The code is completely self-contained and can be compiled and unit-tested\n//! in isolation.  In the full platform the public interfaces defined here\n//! are re-exported by the *Model Ops* layer.\n//!\n//! This module uses the `ExperimentTracker` trait abstraction for all\n//! experiment tracking operations, enabling easy swapping of backends\n//! and mock-based testing.\n//!\n//! Dependencies\n//! ------------\n//! The following third-party crates are required in `Cargo.toml`.\n//!\n//! ```toml\n//! [dependencies]\n//! async-trait = \"0.1\"\n//! rand        = \"0.8\"\n//! thiserror   = \"1.0\"\n//! tokio       = { version = \"1\", features = [\"full\"] }\n//! log         = \"0.4\"\n//! ```\n//!\n//! Note: In production, all blocking‐IO should be pushed to separate worker\n//! pools; here tokio is used for concise asynchronous examples.\n\nuse std::collections::HashMap;\nuse std::path::Path;\nuse std::sync::{Arc, RwLock};\nuse std::time::{Duration, SystemTime};\n\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse log::{debug, error, info, warn};\nuse rand::distributions::{Distribution, Uniform};\nuse rand::rngs::ThreadRng;\nuse thiserror::Error;\nuse tokio::task;\nuse tokio::time::sleep;\n\nuse crate::experiment_tracker::ExperimentTracker;\n\n//\n// Domain Types\n// ------------\n\n/// A unique model identifier.\npub type ModelId = String;\n\n/// A simplistic representation of metric values keyed by their names.\npub type Metrics = HashMap<String, f64>;\n\n/// Hyper-parameter key/value store.  A real implementation would likely use\n/// strongly-typed parameters and ranges.\npub type HyperParams = HashMap<String, f64>;\n\n/// Raw training data handle.  In production this would wrap an Arrow table,\n/// Parquet reader, or a stream of image tensors.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub struct DataBatch {\n    pub num_samples: usize,\n}\n\n//\n// Error Handling\n// --------------\n\n#[derive(Debug, Error)]\npub enum ModelOpsError {\n    #[error(\"model `{0}` not found\")]\n    ModelNotFound(ModelId),\n\n    #[error(\"failed to spawn background task: {0}\")]\n    TaskSpawnError(String),\n\n    #[error(\"training failed: {0}\")]\n    TrainingFailed(String),\n\n    #[error(\"registry error: {0}\")]\n    Registry(String),\n}\n\n//\n//  Model Registry\n// ---------------\n\n/// Metadata for a single, immutable model version.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub struct ModelVersion {\n    pub version: u64,\n    pub trained_at: SystemTime,\n    pub hyper_params: HyperParams,\n    pub metrics: Metrics,\n}\n\n/// Thread-safe, in-memory implementation of a model registry.\n///\n/// In production this would be backed by a SQL/NoSQL database with audit\n/// logging. All writes are versioned and immutable.\n#[derive(Debug, Default, Clone)]\npub struct ModelRegistry {\n    inner: Arc<RwLock<HashMap<ModelId, Vec<ModelVersion>>>>,\n}\n\nimpl ModelRegistry {\n    /// Registers a new model version and returns the assigned version number.\n    pub fn register(\n        &self,\n        id: &ModelId,\n        hyper_params: HyperParams,\n        metrics: Metrics,\n    ) -> Result<u64, ModelOpsError> {\n        let mut guard = self\n            .inner\n            .write()\n            .map_err(|e| ModelOpsError::Registry(e.to_string()))?;\n\n        let versions = guard.entry(id.clone()).or_default();\n        let version_num = versions\n            .last()\n            .map(|v| v.version + 1)\n            .unwrap_or(1);\n\n        versions.push(ModelVersion {\n            version: version_num,\n            trained_at: SystemTime::now(),\n            hyper_params,\n            metrics,\n        });\n\n        info!(\"Registered model `{id}` version {version_num}\");\n        Ok(version_num)\n    }\n\n    /// Returns the latest model version.\n    pub fn latest(&self, id: &ModelId) -> Result<ModelVersion, ModelOpsError> {\n        let guard = self\n            .inner\n            .read()\n            .map_err(|e| ModelOpsError::Registry(e.to_string()))?;\n\n        guard\n            .get(id)\n            .and_then(|v| v.last().cloned())\n            .ok_or_else(|| ModelOpsError::ModelNotFound(id.clone()))\n    }\n}\n\n//\n// Strategy Pattern: Hyper-Parameter Tuners\n// ---------------------------------------\n\n/// Strategies must implement asynchronous tuning given a model id and data.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait HyperParamTuner: Send + Sync {\n    async fn tune(\n        &self,\n        model_id: &ModelId,\n        data: &DataBatch,\n    ) -> Result<HyperParams, ModelOpsError>;\n}\n\n/// A simple grid search tuner.\npub struct GridSearchTuner {\n    learning_rates: Vec<f64>,\n    num_layers: Vec<f64>,\n}\n\nimpl GridSearchTuner {\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            learning_rates: vec![1e-4, 1e-3, 1e-2],\n            num_layers: vec![2.0, 3.0, 4.0],\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl HyperParamTuner for GridSearchTuner {\n    async fn tune(\n        &self,\n        model_id: &ModelId,\n        data: &DataBatch,\n    ) -> Result<HyperParams, ModelOpsError> {\n        info!(\n            \"Running grid search for model `{}` on {} samples\",\n            model_id, data.num_samples\n        );\n\n        // Dummy evaluation metric: random value ∈ [0,1]\n        let mut best_metric = f64::MIN;\n        let mut best_params = HyperParams::new();\n\n        for lr in &self.learning_rates {\n            for layers in &self.num_layers {\n                let simulated_metric = 1.0 / (lr * layers); // deterministic but arbitrary\n                if simulated_metric > best_metric {\n                    best_metric = simulated_metric;\n                    best_params.insert(\"learning_rate\".into(), *lr);\n                    best_params.insert(\"num_layers\".into(), *layers);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        info!(\"Grid search selected params: {:?}\", best_params);\n        Ok(best_params)\n    }\n}\n\n/// A random search tuner.\npub struct RandomSearchTuner {\n    trials: usize,\n    rng: ThreadRng,\n}\n\nimpl RandomSearchTuner {\n    pub fn new(trials: usize) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            trials,\n            rng: rand::thread_rng(),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl HyperParamTuner for RandomSearchTuner {\n    async fn tune(\n        &self,\n        model_id: &ModelId,\n        data: &DataBatch,\n    ) -> Result<HyperParams, ModelOpsError> {\n        info!(\n            \"Running random search for model `{}` with {} trials\",\n            model_id, self.trials\n        );\n\n        let lr_dist = Uniform::new(1e-4, 1e-2);\n        let layer_dist = Uniform::new_inclusive(2.0, 6.0);\n\n        let mut best_metric = f64::MIN;\n        let mut best_params = HyperParams::new();\n\n        for _ in 0..self.trials {\n            let lr = lr_dist.sample(&mut self.rng);\n            let layers = layer_dist.sample(&mut self.rng);\n            let simulated_metric = 1.0 / (lr * layers);\n\n            if simulated_metric > best_metric {\n                best_metric = simulated_metric;\n                best_params.insert(\"learning_rate\".into(), lr);\n                best_params.insert(\"num_layers\".into(), layers);\n            }\n        }\n\n        info!(\"Random search selected params: {:?}\", best_params);\n        Ok(best_params)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Factory for obtaining the right tuner based on contextual information.\n///\n/// In production this might inspect the model type, data modality, or\n/// tenancy metadata.\npub enum TunerKind {\n    GridSearch,\n    RandomSearch { trials: usize },\n}\n\npub fn tuner_factory(kind: TunerKind) -> Arc<dyn HyperParamTuner> {\n    match kind {\n        TunerKind::GridSearch => Arc::new(GridSearchTuner::new()),\n        TunerKind::RandomSearch { trials } => Arc::new(RandomSearchTuner::new(trials)),\n    }\n}\n\n//\n// Observer Pattern: Metrics Events & Subscriptions\n// -----------------------------------------------\n\n/// A metrics event emitted by the serving layer.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub struct MetricsEvent {\n    pub model_id: ModelId,\n    pub metrics: Metrics,\n}\n\n/// Observer trait used by any consumer interested in metrics events.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait Observer: Send + Sync {\n    async fn update(&self, event: MetricsEvent);\n}\n\n/// Subject trait implemented by event producers.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait Subject {\n    async fn add_observer(&self, observer: Arc<dyn Observer>);\n    async fn notify(&self, event: MetricsEvent);\n}\n\n/// Model monitor aggregates runtime metrics and notifies observers when\n/// degradation is detected.\n#[derive(Default)]\npub struct ModelMonitor {\n    observers: Arc<RwLock<Vec<Arc<dyn Observer>>>>,\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl Subject for ModelMonitor {\n    async fn add_observer(&self, observer: Arc<dyn Observer>) {\n        let mut guard = self\n            .observers\n            .write()\n            .expect(\"poisoned lock in ModelMonitor\");\n        guard.push(observer);\n    }\n\n    async fn notify(&self, event: MetricsEvent) {\n        debug!(\"Notifying {} observers\", self.observers.read().unwrap().len());\n        for obs in self.observers.read().unwrap().iter() {\n            obs.update(event.clone()).await;\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n//\n// Experiment Tracking Integration\n// -------------------------------\n\n/// Log hyperparameters to an experiment tracker.\n///\n/// This function accepts any type that implements the `ExperimentTracker` trait,\n/// allowing for easy testing with mocks and swapping of tracking backends.\npub fn log_hyperparams<T: ExperimentTracker>(\n    tracker: &T,\n    params: &HyperParams,\n) {\n    for (name, value) in params {\n        tracker.log_hyperparameter(name, &value.to_string());\n    }\n}\n\n/// Log training metrics to an experiment tracker.\n///\n/// Records each metric in the provided map at the specified step.\npub fn log_training_metrics<T: ExperimentTracker>(\n    tracker: &T,\n    metrics: &Metrics,\n    step: u64,\n) {\n    for (name, value) in metrics {\n        tracker.log_scalar(name, *value, step);\n    }\n}\n\n/// Upload a model artifact using the experiment tracker.\n///\n/// Delegates to the tracker's `upload_model_artifact` method.\npub fn upload_artifact<T: ExperimentTracker>(\n    tracker: &T,\n    local_path: &Path,\n    remote_name: &str,\n) -> Result<(), std::io::Error> {\n    tracker.upload_model_artifact(local_path, remote_name)\n}\n\n//\n// Automated Retrainer\n// -------------------\n\n/// Retrains a model when metrics fall below a dynamic threshold.\npub struct AutoRetrainer<T: ExperimentTracker + 'static> {\n    registry: ModelRegistry,\n    tuner: Arc<dyn HyperParamTuner>,\n    degradation_threshold: f64,\n    tracker: Arc<T>,\n}\n\nimpl<T: ExperimentTracker + 'static> AutoRetrainer<T> {\n    pub fn new(\n        registry: ModelRegistry,\n        tuner: Arc<dyn HyperParamTuner>,\n        degradation_threshold: f64,\n        tracker: Arc<T>,\n    ) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            registry,\n            tuner,\n            degradation_threshold,\n            tracker,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Actual training routine.  In production this would call into a training\n    /// service, ship GPU jobs, etc.\n    async fn train_model(\n        &self,\n        model_id: &ModelId,\n        hyper_params: HyperParams,\n        data: DataBatch,\n    ) -> Result<Metrics, ModelOpsError> {\n        info!(\"Training model `{model_id}` with params {:?}\", hyper_params);\n\n        // Log hyperparameters to tracker\n        log_hyperparams(self.tracker.as_ref(), &hyper_params);\n\n        // Fake training latency\n        sleep(Duration::from_millis(500)).await;\n\n        // Return fake metrics inversely proportional to learning rate\n        let val_accuracy = 0.85\n            + 0.1 * (1e-2 - hyper_params[\"learning_rate\"]).clamp(0.0, 1e-2) / 1e-2;\n\n        let metrics = HashMap::from([(\"val_accuracy\".into(), val_accuracy)]);\n\n        // Log training metrics\n        log_training_metrics(self.tracker.as_ref(), &metrics, 1);\n\n        Ok(metrics)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Run a complete training pipeline with experiment tracking.\n///\n/// This function coordinates hyperparameter tuning, model training, and\n/// result logging through the provided `ExperimentTracker`.\npub async fn run_training_pipeline<T: ExperimentTracker>(\n    tracker: &T,\n    tuner: Arc<dyn HyperParamTuner>,\n    model_id: &ModelId,\n    data: DataBatch,\n    registry: &ModelRegistry,\n) -> Result<u64, ModelOpsError> {\n    // Step 1: Hyperparameter tuning\n    let params = tuner.tune(model_id, &data).await?;\n    log_hyperparams(tracker, &params);\n\n    // Step 2: Train model (simulated)\n    info!(\"Training model `{model_id}` with params {:?}\", params);\n    let val_accuracy = 0.85 + 0.1 * (1e-2 - params[\"learning_rate\"]).clamp(0.0, 1e-2) / 1e-2;\n    let metrics = HashMap::from([(\"val_accuracy\".into(), val_accuracy)]);\n\n    // Step 3: Log metrics\n    log_training_metrics(tracker, &metrics, 1);\n\n    // Step 4: Register model\n    let version = registry.register(model_id, params, metrics)?;\n\n    info!(\"Model `{model_id}` trained and registered as version {version}\");\n    Ok(version)\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl<T: ExperimentTracker + Send + Sync + 'static> Observer for AutoRetrainer<T> {\n    async fn update(&self, event: MetricsEvent) {\n        let acc = event.metrics.get(\"val_accuracy\").cloned().unwrap_or(1.0);\n        if acc >= self.degradation_threshold {\n            debug!(\n                \"Model `{}` accuracy {:.3} above threshold {:.3}, no action.\",\n                event.model_id, acc, self.degradation_threshold\n            );\n            return;\n        }\n\n        warn!(\n            \"Model `{}` accuracy {:.3} below threshold {:.3}, initiating retraining.\",\n            event.model_id, acc, self.degradation_threshold\n        );\n\n        let registry_clone = self.registry.clone();\n        let tuner_clone = self.tuner.clone();\n        let model_id = event.model_id.clone();\n        let tracker_clone = Arc::clone(&self.tracker);\n\n        // Fire-and-forget background task\n        if let Err(e) = task::spawn(async move {\n            // Step 1: Pull fresh data — here we fake it\n            let data = DataBatch { num_samples: 10_000 };\n\n            // Step 2: Hyper-parameter tuning\n            let tuned_params = tuner_clone.tune(&model_id, &data).await?;\n\n            // Log hyperparameters using the tracker\n            log_hyperparams(tracker_clone.as_ref(), &tuned_params);\n\n            // Step 3: Train\n            let new_metrics = train_model_static(\n                &model_id,\n                tuned_params.clone(),\n                data,\n                tracker_clone.as_ref(),\n            ).await?;\n\n            // Step 4: Register new version\n            registry_clone\n                .register(&model_id, tuned_params, new_metrics)\n                .map(|v| info!(\"Model `{}` retrained, new version {}\", model_id, v))\n        })\n        .await\n        {\n            error!(\"Failed to spawn retraining task: {e}\");\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// A static helper so we can move it into the spawned async task easily.\nasync fn train_model_static<T: ExperimentTracker>(\n    model_id: &ModelId,\n    hyper_params: HyperParams,\n    _data: DataBatch,\n    tracker: &T,\n) -> Result<Metrics, ModelOpsError> {\n    info!(\"Training model `{model_id}` with params {:?}\", hyper_params);\n\n    // Log hyperparameters\n    log_hyperparams(tracker, &hyper_params);\n\n    // Fake training latency\n    sleep(Duration::from_millis(500)).await;\n\n    // Return fake metrics inversely proportional to learning rate\n    let val_accuracy = 0.85\n        + 0.1 * (1e-2 - hyper_params[\"learning_rate\"]).clamp(0.0, 1e-2) / 1e-2;\n\n    let metrics = HashMap::from([(\"val_accuracy\".into(), val_accuracy)]);\n\n    // Log training metrics\n    log_training_metrics(tracker, &metrics, 1);\n\n    Ok(metrics)\n}\n\n//\n// Example Usage\n// -------------\n//! To keep the single-file constraint, an example `main` is included behind\n//! the `demo` feature flag.  Compile via `cargo run --features demo`.\n#[cfg(feature = \"demo\")]\n#[tokio::main]\nasync fn main() -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    use crate::experiment_tracker::DefaultTracker;\n\n    env_logger::init();\n\n    // Create the experiment tracker\n    let tracker = Arc::new(DefaultTracker::new(\"http://localhost:5000\"));\n\n    // Registry bootstrap\n    let registry = ModelRegistry::default();\n    registry.register(\n        &\"detector_rgb\".into(),\n        HashMap::from([\n            (\"learning_rate\".into(), 1e-3),\n            (\"num_layers\".into(), 3.0),\n        ]),\n        HashMap::from([(\"val_accuracy\".into(), 0.91)]),\n    )?;\n\n    // Monitoring & automated retraining\n    let monitor = ModelMonitor::default();\n    let tuner = tuner_factory(TunerKind::RandomSearch { trials: 20 });\n    let retrainer = Arc::new(AutoRetrainer::new(\n        registry.clone(),\n        tuner,\n        0.92, // Threshold\n        Arc::clone(&tracker),\n    ));\n    monitor.add_observer(retrainer).await;\n\n    // Emit some events – normally this comes from the serving layer.\n    let events = model_id: \"detector_rgb\".into(),\n            metrics: HashMap::from([(\"val_accuracy\".into(), 0.89)]),\n        },\n    ];\n\n    for ev in events {\n        monitor.notify(ev).await;\n        sleep(Duration::from_millis(100)).await;\n    }\n\n    // Wait for background retraining\n    sleep(Duration::from_secs(2)).await;\n\n    // Check registry\n    let latest =
